          -d that drives it. The equal-weight                            
      189 -   average of the hazards is reported next to it as a secondar
          -y **multi-hazard** score.                                     
      190 -   Levels: low < 33 ≤ moderate < 66 ≤ high.                   
      121 +## 4. Storage and ingestion                                   
      122 
      123 +**Why SQLite.** One file, no server, nothing to install; ~44k 
          +daily rows for 12 hubs × 10 years is                          
      124 +small. The SQL is plain, so moving to Postgres means changing 
          +the connection, not the queries. The                          
      125 +agent and tools only read; ingestion and score snapshots are t
          +he only writers.                                              
      126 +                                                              
      127 +| Table | Content |                                           
      128 +|---|---|                                                     
      129 +| `hubs` | Synced from `data/hubs.json` (removed hubs are dele
          +ted with their data) |                                        
      130 +| `daily_weather` | One row per hub per local calendar day: `s
          +now_cm`, `tmin_c`, `precip_mm` (ACIS), `gust_kmh` (Open-Meteo)
          +, and the ingestion run that wrote each source |              
      131 +| `nri` | `(hub_id, field) → value` for the hub's county; NULL
          + kept as NULL |                                               
      132 +| `ingestion_runs` | Source, time, status, rows written, data-
          +quality details (trace / missing counts, Open-Meteo grid cell)
          + |                                                            
      133 +| `score_runs`, `hub_scores` | Timestamped score snapshots wit
          +h a hash of the scoring settings (for a future score-change al
          +ert) |                                                        
      134 +                                                              
      135 +- **Raw daily rows, not aggregates**, so any metric or thresho
          +ld can be recomputed without re-fetching                      
      136 +  (this is also what makes seasonal scores possible later).   
      137 +- **Idempotent**: every write is an upsert keyed on `(hub_id, 
          +date)` or `(hub_id, field)`. Station and                      
      138 +  gust columns are written by separate steps, so each source c
          +an be re-run alone.                                           
      139 +- Units are stored metric (inches → cm/mm, °F → °C).          
      140 +                                                              
      141 +## 5. Scoring                                                 
      142 +                                                              
      143 +All settings live in one file, `backend/scoring/config.py`, ea
          +ch with its rationale; they are                               
      144 +assumptions the client can tune.                              
      145 +                                                              
      146 +1. **Raw metrics** over the 10-year window 2016–2025 (one year
          + is too noisy for risk).                                      
      147 +2. **Fixed-anchor normalization** to 0–100: `lo → 0`, `hi → 10
          +0`, linear, clamped. Anchors are set                          
      148 +   from references *outside* our hubs, so scores do not depend
          + on which hubs were picked and stay                           
      149 +   comparable over time: station metrics from well-known extre
          +me US stations (same code, same years);                       
      150 +   NRI metrics by one rule, **100 = the US county 99th percent
          +ile**.                                                        
      151 +3. **Hazard score** = weighted average of its metrics.        
      152 +4. **Overall score = the worst single hazard**, and hubs are r
          +anked by it: a hub closes when one hazard                     
      153 +   hits it, not on an average. The equal-weight average of the
          + three hazards is shown next to it as a                       
      154 +   secondary **multi-hazard** score (breadth of exposure).    
      155 +5. **Levels**: low < 33 ≤ moderate < 66 ≤ high.

      234 -## 6. Assumptions & limitations                               
      235 -- **Hub list is assumed** (client gave none): 12 hubs in `data
          -/hubs.json`, chosen to cover every city                       
      236 -  in the example questions, ≥ 5 Midwest hubs, real logistics c
          -entres, and a spread of hazard profiles.                      
      237 -- **Hub location = the main-airport NWS climate station** (coo
          -rdinates from ACIS metadata, see                              
      238 -  `data/hubs.json`). Real warehouse addresses would replace th
          -is; the nearest long-record station                           
      239 -  would then be chosen per warehouse.                         
      240 -- **"Last year" = calendar year** (Jan–Dec). NWS NOWData shows
          - snow seasons (Jul–Jun) by default, so                        
      241 -  season totals there will not match our calendar-year figures
          -; monthly sums do.                                            
      242 -- ACIS codes: `T` (trace) counts as 0 for every threshold; `M`
          - (missing) is stored as NULL and                              
      243 -  excluded from both the count and the number of observed days
          -.                                                             
      244 -- Open-Meteo returns the nearest model grid cell, not the exac
          -t point; we store the returned coordinates.                   
      245 -- **NRI county size.** Stage 2 suspected that inland-flood fre
          -quency scales with county area (Cook                          
      246 -  5.9 vs DuPage 2.8 events/yr). The national check in Stage 3 
          -does not support this for flood                               
      247 -  (Spearman −0.01) but does for tornado (+0.34), so tornado fr
          -equency — context only — is higher in                         
      248 -  large counties. Maricopa (Phoenix) is the top US county for 
          -inland-flood frequency although its                           
      249 -  measured heavy-rain days are the lowest of our hubs; NRI flo
          -od has a low weight and the agent should                      
      250 -  mention this caveat for Phoenix.                            
      251 -- **Worst-hazard overall** ignores breadth: a hub moderately e
          -xposed to two hazards can rank below                          
      252 -  a hub with one slightly higher hazard. The multi-hazard scor
          -e is shown next to it for that reason.                        
      253 -  A combined method (e.g. probability any hazard disrupts the 
          -hub) would need client disruption data.                       
      254 -- Open-Meteo free tier is for non-commercial use; a production
          - deployment needs the commercial plan.                        
      255 -- Open-Meteo is reanalysis/model data, not measurements (see s
          -ection 3); wind gusts are model gusts and                     
      256 -  understate thunderstorm and hurricane gusts.                
      257 -- NRI is a county-level, long-term estimate; large counties (M
          -aricopa, Harris, Cook) aggregate a lot                        
      258 -  of area.                                                    
      259 -- Extreme cold ≤ −15 °C is an explicit assumption (no national
          - standard).                                                   
      185 +Each hub is placed at its **main-airport NWS climate station**
          + (e.g. Denver → KDEN), and *all* its                          
      186 +weather data comes from that one point. Its NRI county is the 
          +county containing the station point                           
      187 +(Chicago → DuPage, Kansas City → Platte, Atlanta → Clayton; th
          +e other 9 match the city's county).                           
      188 +Hubs have aliases ("DFW", "Twin Cities", "Kansas City, MO"). R
          +eplacing `hubs.json` with the client's                        
      189 +real sites and re-running ingestion is the only change needed.
      190 
      261 -## 7. Future work                                             
      262 -- **Seasonal / monthly scores** (e.g. "which hub is safest in 
          -the fall?"). Doable without new data,                         
      263 -  because daily rows are stored: compute the same metrics per 
          -month or season. Today the agent says                         
      264 -  there is no seasonal data, offers the year-round ranking and
          - sets confidence low (user-tested).                           
      265 -- Persist sessions (SQLite) so conversations survive a restart
          -; streaming responses in the UI.                              
      266 -- Tie numbers in the answer text to the hub each sentence is a
          -bout (today: any hub's tool results).                         
      267 -- A ranking tool for context hazards (tornado / hail / ice sto
          -rm); today the agent makes one                                
      268 -  `get_hub_metric` call per hub (in parallel, one round).     
      269 -- NOAA GHCN-Daily / ISD for measured peak gusts (hourly ISD ha
          -s gusts; daily summaries do not).                             
      270 -- Calibrate thresholds and weights against the client's histor
          -ical disruption records.                                      
      191 +## 7. Agent                                                   
      192 +                                                              
      193 +### 7.1 Tools                                                 
      194 +All tools are `strict: true` (schema-valid arguments guarantee
          +d) and use enums for hazards, regions and                     
      195 +metrics, so out-of-scope values (e.g. "earthquake") cannot be 
          +passed in.                                                    
      196 +                                                              
      197 +| Tool | Returns |                                            
      198 +|---|---|                                                     
      199 +| `list_hubs(region?)` | Hubs, regions, station and county |  
      200 +| `rank_hubs(hazard, region?)` | Ranking by overall, multi-haz
          +ard or one hazard; equal scores share a rank; `points_behind_p
          +revious` per hub |                                            
      201 +| `compare_hubs(hubs[], hazards[]?)` | Side-by-side scores wit
          +h metric breakdowns and caveats |                             
      202 +| `get_hub_metric(hub, metric, year?)` | One factual metric fo
          +r one year or the 10-year window: qualifying days, observed da
          +ys, % of observed days, coverage, threshold, source |         
      203 +| `explain_score(hub, hazard?)` | Full breakdown, rank, not-sc
          +ored context (tornado / hail / ice storm), caveats |          
      204 +| `submit_answer(...)` | The response contract (below) |      
      205 +                                                              
      206 +Tools return units, thresholds, period, coverage and source wi
          +th every number, and return                                   
      207 +`not_found` / `no_data` as normal results, never exceptions. *
          +*Caveats are generated from the data**,                       
      208 +not hard-coded per hub: county NRI far above the station data 
          +(Phoenix flood), two hazards within 3                         
      209 +points (Detroit), coverage below 99 %, tornado counts growing 
          +with county size.                                             
      210 +                                                              
      211 +### 7.2 Response contract                                     
      212 +`submit_answer` input, validated with Pydantic (`backend/agent
          +/schema.py`):                                                 
      213 +`{answer, hubs_referenced[], scores[{hub_id, name, value, unit
          +}], reasoning, assumptions[],                                 
      214 +confidence: high|medium|low, data_sources[]}`.                
      215 +                                                              
      216 +### 7.3 Guarantees enforced in code                           
      217 +- **The answer is always structured.** Current Claude models r
          +eject forced tool choice, so the prompt                       
      218 +  asks for `submit_answer` and the loop checks it: a reply wit
          +hout it gets one reminder; a second miss                      
      219 +  is a clear error. Invalid input gets one retry, then an erro
          +r.                                                            
      220 +- **Numbers are grounded.** Every value in `scores[]` must app
          +ear in a tool result for that hub in the                      
      221 +  session (rounding tolerance only); one retry, then the answe
          +r is returned flagged. Every number in                        
      222 +  the answer *text* is also matched against tool results, the 
          +system prompt (thresholds, years, hub                         
      223 +  count) and the question; unmatched numbers are returned in `
          +grounding.text_unverified` and shown as                       
      224 +  a warning in the UI (warning only: never blocks).           
      225 +- **Arithmetic stays in code.** When the text check caught the
          + model subtracting two scores itself                          
      226 +  ("only 2.7 points separate them"), the fix was a tool field 
          +(`points_behind_previous`) plus a prompt                      
      227 +  rule to quote it — the root cause, not just the warning.    
      228 +- **History is append-only** (required for prompt caching and 
          +for thinking blocks to stay valid). A                         
      229 +  failed turn is cut off the end of the session so it cannot b
          +reak the next question.                                       
      230 +- Limits: at most 8 model calls per question; errors from tool
          +s go back to the model, not up the stack.                     
      231 +                                                              
      232 +### 7.4 Prompt, caching and model settings                    
      233 +- The system prompt (`backend/agent/prompt.py`) is built once 
          +from `hubs.json` and settings: scope,                         
      234 +  how scores work, and rules — code computes, say "no data" in
          +stead of guessing, check the premise of                       
      235 +  the question, state thresholds and periods, quote caveats an
          +d gaps, never mention tool or field names.                    
      236 +- When an answer references 2+ hubs, the UI shows the scores t
          +able, so the prompt asks for ~3 short                         
      237 +  sentences in that case (the conclusion plus what the table d
          +oes not show). The same constant                              
      238 +  (`TABLE_MIN_HUBS`) drives the prompt and the UI.            
      239 +- **Prompt caching**: one breakpoint after the system prompt (
          +tools + system ≈ 3.7k tokens, shared by                       
      240 +  every question) and one automatic breakpoint at the end of t
          +he history.                                                   
      241 +- `.env` settings: `AGENT_MODEL` (default `claude-sonnet-5-5`)
          +, `AGENT_EFFORT` (default `medium`),                          
      242 +  `AGENT_THINKING` (`adaptive`, or `between_tools` on Sonnet 5
          +.5 only — validated at startup),                              
      243 +  `AGENT_FALLBACKS` (server-side refusal fallback, on).       
      244 +- Sessions are kept in memory per `session_id` and lost on res
          +tart.                                                         
      245 +                                                              
      246 +## 8. Chat UI                                                 
      247 +                                                              
      248 +`frontend/` — Vite + React + TypeScript, no UI library, plain 
          +CSS. Screenshots with captions:                               
      249 +[`docs/screenshots/README.md`](screenshots/README.md).        
      250 +                                                              
      251 +- **Answer card**: the answer first; a **hub scores table** (w
          +inter, hurricane, flood, overall,                             
      252 +  multi-hazard) with the worst hazard outlined; a confidence b
          +adge; a **"How I got this"** line listing                     
      253 +  the data tools called and the time taken; a **warning** when
          + the grounding check reports numbers no                       
      254 +  tool returned; collapsible (closed) figures cited, assumptio
          +ns and data sources.                                          
      255 +- The table is filled by the backend from the scoring code (`h
          +ub_scores` in the `/chat` response), never                    
      256 +  by the model. It is open by default for rankings and compari
          +sons (2+ hubs) and behind a show/hide                         
      257 +  toggle for single-hub answers.                              
      258 +- Example-question chips on the empty screen, follow-ups in th
          +e same session, a "New conversation"                          
      259 +  button, and a loading card with stage messages, elapsed seco
          +nds and bouncing dots (static under                           
      260 +  reduced motion).                                            
      261 +- **Colour with text labels**: levels green / amber / red, haz
          +ards blue / purple / teal; colour always                      
      262 +  sits next to a word, so it works without colour vision. **Li
          +ght and dark themes** on CSS variables,                       
      263 +  starting from the system setting and remembering the user's 
          +choice; every text colour pair meets                          
      264 +  WCAG AA contrast (≥ 4.5:1) in both themes.                  
      265 +                                                              
      266 +## 9. Evaluation                                              
      267 +                                                              
      268 +`python eval/run_eval.py` runs `eval/cases.yaml` against the a
          +gent in-process (same code path as                            
      269 +`/chat`). 19 cases / 21 questions:                            
      270 +                                                              
      271 +| Group | Cases |                                             
      272 +|---|---|                                                     
      273 +| Client examples (4) | Midwest winter, Miami vs Houston, Denv
          +er snowfall %, Dallas false premise |                         
      274 +| Follow-ups (2) | "And how does Kansas City compare on flood?
          +"; South ranking → "Which of *them*…" |                       
      275 +| Out of scope (7) | Seattle, earthquakes, 2010, safest in the
          + fall, next week's forecast, money lost, prompt injection |   
      276 +| Judgment (4) | Kansas City / Atlanta flood tie, Detroit near
          + tie, "Phoenix is obviously the riskiest, right?", invest in 3
          + hubs |                                                       
      277 +| Aliases (2) | DFW → Dallas, Twin Cities → Minneapolis |     
      278 +                                                              
      279 +**How it checks — all code, no LLM judge.** Every case: a sche
          +ma-valid answer, grounded numbers, no                         
      280 +unverified numbers in the text, no tool / field names in the t
          +ext. Per case: tools called, hubs                             
      281 +referenced, which hub is named first (or the first k), require
          +d numbers, required / forbidden wording                       
      282 +(regex), no numbers for out-of-scope questions, confidence. **
          +Expected values are computed** with the                       
      283 +agent's own tool functions (e.g. "the top hub of `rank_hubs(wi
          +nter, Midwest)`"), and **preconditions**                      
      284 +(e.g. "Kansas City and Atlanta are still tied") make a case sk
          +ip rather than fail wrongly after a data                      
      285 +refresh. `--dry-run` shows the expected values for free; `--bu
          +dget` caps the spend of a run.                                
      286 +`tests/test_eval_checks.py` shows the checks fail on deliberat
          +ely bad answers.                                              
      287 +                                                              
      288 +**Results** (2026-11-22, every answer saved in `eval/results/`
          +):                                                            
      289 +                                                              
      290 +| Setting | Cases | Passed | Median / max per question | Cost 
          +|                                                             
      291 +|---|---|---|---|---|                                         
      292 +| **Sonnet 5.5, effort medium (default)** | 19 | **19/19** | 5
          +.7 s / 9.7 s | $0.23 |                                        
      293 +| Sonnet 5.5, effort low | 19 | 17/19, 19/19 after a prompt fi
          +x | 5.8 s / 14.1 s | $0.22 |                                  
      294 +| Opus 5.5, effort medium | 6 hardest | 6/6 | 13.3 s / 20.3 s 
          +| $0.27 |                                                     
      295 +                                                              
      296 +Low effort was not faster (most time is spent writing the answ
          +er, not thinking). Opus passed the hard                       
      297 +cases too, at about twice the latency and three times the cost
          +. Sonnet 5.5 at medium effort is the default.                 
      298 +                                                              
      299 +## 10. Assumptions, uncertainty and limitations               
      300 +                                                              
      301 +**Scope**                                                     
      302 +- The **hub list is assumed** and hubs are placed at **airport
          + weather stations**, not real warehouse                       
      303 +  addresses (section 6).                                      
      304 +- Three hazards are scored (winter, hurricane, flood). Tornado
          +, hail and ice storm are context only;                        
      305 +  earthquakes, wildfire, heat and others are out of scope.    
      306 +- No seasonal or monthly scores, no forecasts, no financial lo
          +sses: the agent says so instead of guessing.                  
      307 +- **"Last year" = calendar year 2025** (Jan–Dec). NWS NOWData 
          +shows Jul–Jun snow seasons by default,                        
      308 +  so season totals there will not match.                      
      309 +                                                              
      310 +**Data**                                                      
      311 +- One airport station per hub: conditions at a warehouse elsew
          +here in the metro can differ.                                 
      312 +- **Wind gusts are modelled** (Open-Meteo reanalysis), smooth 
          +out thunderstorm and hurricane peaks, and                     
      313 +  are therefore not scored. Open-Meteo's free tier is non-comm
          +ercial; production needs the paid plan.                       
      314 +- **NRI is county-level and long-term.** Large counties aggreg
          +ate a lot of area: Maricopa (Phoenix) is                      
      315 +  the top US county for inland-flood frequency although Phoeni
          +x has the fewest measured heavy-rain days                     
      316 +  of our hubs, so NRI flood has a low weight (0.2) and the age
          +nt states this caveat. Tornado counts grow                    
      317 +  with county size (rank correlation +0.34 across US counties)
          +; flood counts do not (−0.01), so flood                       
      318 +  frequency is not divided by area.                           
      319 +- Missing station days are excluded, not treated as zero; cove
          +rage is ≥ 97 % for every hub, and no                          
      320 +  missing snowfall day had both precipitation and a minimum te
          +mperature at or below 1 °C.                                   
      321 +                                                              
      322 +**Method**                                                    
      323 +- **Thresholds and weights are expert assumptions, not calibra
          +ted** against the client's disruption                         
      324 +  history: −15 °C for extreme cold has no national standard; 1
          + in of snow and 25 mm of rain are common                      
      325 +  but not universal disruption thresholds. All live in `backen
          +d/scoring/config.py`.                                         
      326 +- Anchors at national extremes mean no hub may reach "high" on
          + a hazard even if it is the worst of                          
      327 +  our 12 (Minneapolis winter is "moderate"; Duluth and Buffalo
          + are worse). That is intentional.                             
      328 +- **Overall = worst hazard** ignores breadth: a hub moderately
          + exposed to two hazards can rank below one                    
      329 +  with a single, slightly higher hazard. The multi-hazard scor
          +e is shown for that reason.                                   
      330 +- Combining NRI national percentiles with station-based linear
          + scales in one average is a modelling                         
      331 +  choice, not a statistical identity.                         
      332 +                                                              
      333 +**Agent**                                                     
      334 +- The grounding check matches numbers in the answer text again
          +st *any* hub's tool results, not the                          
      335 +  hub the sentence is about, and does not detect numbers writt
          +en as words.                                                  
      336 +- Answers vary slightly between runs; the eval checks structur
          +e, numbers and wording, not exact text.                       
      337 +- Sessions live in memory and are lost when the backend restar
          +ts.                                                           
      338 +                                                              
      339 +## 11. Decisions log                                          
      340 +                                                              
      341 +All decisions were made on 2026-11-22 with the user, stage by 
          +stage (see `docs/sessions/`).                                 
      342 +                                                              
      343 +| # | Decision | Why |                                        
      344 +|---|---|---|                                                 
      345 +| D1 | Scores use a **10-year average (2016–2025)**; factual "
          +last year" questions use 2025. | One year is too noisy (a hurr
          +icane year or a freeze year swings the score). |              
      346 +| D2 | NRI **frequency** fields, not `*_RISKS`. | `*_RISKS` in
          +cludes dollar exposure and social vulnerability and saturates 
          +(inland flood 95–100 for all 12 hubs; Dallas winter 98.7 > Min
          +neapolis 97.4). |                                             
      347 +| D3 | NRI **tornado, hail, ice storm** stored as context, not
          + scored. | Lets the Dallas answer cite data (hail 96.9th, torn
          +ado 96.4th US county percentile). |                           
      348 +| D4 | Project moved out of OneDrive; git repo; one commit per
          + stage. | OneDrive sync locks SQLite and churns on `node_modul
          +es`; reviewable history. |                                    
      349 +| D5 | Always show the **worst single hazard** with the overal
          +l score. | An average hides one extreme hazard (Miami: high hu
          +rricane, zero winter). |                                      
      350 +| D6 | Model is configurable; default **Claude Sonnet 5.5**. |
          + Demo latency; the model only routes and explains, code comput
          +es. Confirmed by D16. |                                       
      351 +| D7 | **ACIS station data** for snow, temperature, precipitat
          +ion; **Open-Meteo for gusts only**, at the same station coordi
          +nates; hubs at airport stations. | Modelled data misses extrem
          +e days (section 3). One point per hub keeps all metrics compar
          +able. |                                                       
      352 +| D8 | NRI **annualized frequency (`*_AFREQ`)** for all hazard
          +s; low NRI weight for flood. | Easy to explain ("~17 winter ev
          +ents a year"); matches climate expectations for winter and hur
          +ricane. |                                                     
      353 +| D9 | NRI county = county containing the station point. | Sam
          +e-place rule (D7); neighbouring counties differ little for win
          +ter / hurricane / hail / ice. |                               
      354 +| D10 | Missing days excluded from count and denominator; cove
          +rage reported; < 90 % → unknown. | A day with no data is not a
          + day with no snow. |                                          
      355 +| D11 | **Wind gusts removed from the hurricane score**; kept 
          +as context. | Model gusts peak in the Midwest (Columbus 6.2 da
          +ys/yr vs Miami 1.1). |                                        
      356 +| D12 | NRI flood frequency **not divided by county area** (sw
          +itch `FLOOD_NRI_PER_AREA`, off). Reverses an earlier plan. | A
          +cross all US counties flood frequency does not grow with area 
          +(−0.01); dividing pushed small urban counties up (Denver to th
          +e 88th percentile) and Houston down. |                        
      357 +| D13 | 3-day precipitation = **average of each year's maximum
          +**. | Houston's 10-year maximum (722 mm) is Hurricane Harvey a
          +lone. |                                                       
      358 +| D14 | One anchor rule for NRI: 100 = US county 99th percenti
          +le; anchors stay at national extremes. | Consistent, explainab
          +le, independent of which hubs were picked; hardcoded so a data
          + refresh cannot move the scale. |                             
      359 +| D15 | **Overall score = worst single hazard**; hubs ranked b
          +y it; weighted average kept as "multi-hazard". Replaces the br
          +ief's "overall = weighted average". | The client invests per h
          +ub and a hub closes when one hazard hits it. The average compr
          +essed 10 of 12 hubs to "low" (Memphis: flood 70, average 28). 
          +|                                                             
      360 +| D16 | Keep **Sonnet 5.5 at effort medium**; Opus 5.5 and low
          + effort stay config options. | Eval: Sonnet medium 19/19, medi
          +an 5.7 s, $0.23 per run; low effort not faster; Opus ~2× slowe
          +r, ~3× cost, same result on hard cases. |                     
      361 +                                                              
      362 +## 12. Future work                                            
      363 +- **Seasonal / monthly scores** ("which hub is safest in the f
          +all?"): possible without new data,                            
      364 +  because daily rows are stored.                              
      365 +- **Calibrate thresholds and weights** against the client's hi
          +storical disruption records (which hub                        
      366 +  closed, when, why) — the single biggest improvement to the s
          +cores' meaning.                                               
      367 +- Real warehouse locations instead of airport stations; neares
          +t long-record station per site.                               
      368 +- **Score-change alerts**: score snapshots are already stored 
          +with a settings hash; a nightly job would                     
      369 +  recompute, compare and post to a webhook. Live NWS alerts pe
          +r hub (API verified, not wired in).                           
      370 +- Measured peak gusts (NOAA ISD hourly data) so wind can be sc
          +ored.                                                         
      371 +- Persist sessions; stream answers in the UI; tie text numbers
          + to the hub each sentence is about.                           
      372 +- A ranking tool for context hazards (today the agent makes on
          +e metric call per hub, in parallel).                          
      373 +- Deployment (not done in this version).

          weather.db` (~15 s). Safe to re-run. |
      48 -| Backend | `uvicorn backend.app:app --port 8000` | `GET /healt
         -h`, `POST /chat` |

         +GET /health`, `POST /chat` |

          st:5173 (proxies to the backend) |

● Stage 7 is committed (52b24dd), with all three changes in. The working tree is
  clean and all 70 tests pass.

  1. § 7.3, forced tool choice: now tested, not assumed

  I couldn't honestly write "what we saw" as things stood: we had never tried
  it. The original sentence came from Anthropic's documentation, which I read in
  Stage 4 and designed around. So I tested it. Error requests aren't billed, so
  the test cost nothing. Six forced requests all returned HTTP 400:
  - Sonnet 5.5 with adaptive thinking, and with between_tools
  - Opus 5.5
  - each one forcing submit_answer and forcing "any tool"

  The error text is tool_choice: type "tool" and "any" are not supported for
  this model. So the restriction comes from the model, not the thinking setting;
  your proposed wording tied it to the thinking settings, and I've written it
  the way the test came out. § 7.3 now says what was tested, on which date, and
  the exact error. It then explains why the loop asks for submit_answer, checks
  for it, and relies on strict: true for valid arguments.

  2. Cost per question

  I took the figures from the final eval run, checked case by case:
  - $0.23 for 21 questions, about $0.011 per answer on Sonnet 5.5
  - 0.4–0.6 cents when no data lookup is needed
  - 0.8–1.0 cent for a single lookup
  - 1.4–2.1 cents for comparisons and "why" answers
  - Opus 5.5 costs about 3× that

  The headline is in the README's "How it works" section; the full breakdown,
  plus a note on how prompt caching keeps costs down, is in ARCHITECTURE § 9.

  3. No authentication or rate limiting

  This is now in the README's limitations summary and in a new "Operations"
  group in ARCHITECTURE § 10. Anyone who can reach /chat can use it, and spend
  the API credit behind it. It's for local use and not ready for public exposure
  as-is: it would need authentication, per-user rate limits and a spend cap
  first.

● Vercel's 300 s function limit isn't the bottleneck, since our answers take

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

● That check turned up something important: the analysis period is computed from
  today's date (LAST_FULL_YEAR = today.year - 1). A deployment that ships a
  fixed database would break on 1 January 2027. Overnight, "last year" would
  become 2026, the 10-year window would shift to 2017–2026, and every metric
  would come back unknown. The plan fixes that. Docker isn't installed locally,
  which affects how we test.

  My pick is one service on Render: the backend also serves the built frontend.
  The plan, with nothing built yet:

  Where it runs and why

  Part: Backend + built frontend, one service, one URL
  Where: Render web service, built from a Dockerfile in the repo
  Cost: Free while we test. Then Starter, ~$7/month (third-party figure;
    confirm on render.com/pricing) for as long as reviewers have the link, and
    downgrade afterwards
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Part: Claude API
  Where: Anthropic
  Cost: ~$0.01 per answer, protected by a spend cap (below)

  Why one service rather than Vercel plus a backend host. The split works, but
  it adds parts without removing any problem:
  - The backend still needs a long-running host, for the sessions and SQLite
    reasons you gave.
  - Two services means two deploys, two sets of settings, and CORS between them.
  - The access code would have to cross origins.

  With one service, the UI calls /chat on its own origin, exactly as it does
  locally through the Vite proxy. FastAPI already runs there, so serving
  frontend/dist is about five lines.

  Why Render over Railway. Both would work. Render's free tier lets us test the
  real deployment for $0 first, and its config lives in the repo, where a
  reviewer can read it. Railway's Hobby plan ($5/month, including $5 of usage)
  is a fine alternative.

  Your Vercel concerns, checked:
  - Time limit: not a blocker. Vercel's docs give Hobby up to 300 s per
    function, against our 6–20 s answers.
  - SQLite: actually readable from a read-only filesystem. The request path only
    reads, and score snapshots are written by a script, not by /chat.
  - In-memory sessions: the real blocker. Serverless instances don't share
    memory, so follow-ups would randomly lose their context. Fixing that would
    mean adding Redis or a KV store, which is new infrastructure just to work
    around the platform.

  How the SQLite file gets there

  - Commit a database snapshot (3.9 MB) with a .gitignore exception. The
    deployed app then answers from exactly the data the eval, screenshots and
    README describe, and a deploy can't fail because NOAA or Open-Meteo is down
    that day.
  - Pin the period. A new ANALYSIS_LAST_YEAR=2025 setting, used in production,
    stops the date-based period from breaking on 1 January. Locally it stays
    automatic.
  - Render's disk is ephemeral, which is fine because nothing writes to it at
    request time.

  Access code and rate limits

  - The backend checks the code on every /chat request. The UI sends it in an
    X-Access-Code header, the backend compares it in constant time, and a
    missing or wrong code gets 401. The code lives in Render's environment
    settings (ACCESS_CODE), not in the repo.
  - It can't be left off by accident. With APP_ENV=production, the app refuses
    to start without an access code. Locally nothing changes.
  - The UI: a small screen asks for the code once and keeps it in the browser's
    session storage. A 401 sends you back to that screen.
  - Rate limits, in memory, which is fine for a single instance:
    - 20 questions per hour per IP address, read from Render's X-Forwarded-For
      header
    - 60 per hour across everyone
    - 10 wrong codes per hour per IP, to stop guessing
    - Anything over a limit gets 429 with a clear message.
  - I'd add a daily spend cap (say $1/day). The backend already counts tokens
    for every answer, so it can stop answering with "demo budget reached for
    today" before your remaining ~$5 is at risk. It's an extra safety net under
    the rate limits; your call.

  The API key

  - ANTHROPIC_API_KEY goes in Render's environment settings only. .env stays
    git-ignored, as already verified.
  - Checks before sending the link: git grep finds no sk-ant anywhere in the
    repo, and the built JavaScript bundle doesn't contain the key either. The
    browser never sees it.

  Cold starts

  - On the free tier: after 15 minutes idle, the first request waits about 30–60
    s. That's fine for our own testing, but reviewers open links at random
    times, so a one-minute blank page would look broken.
  - For the review window: the paid Starter instance, which doesn't sleep.
  - Before the interview (as well): open the URL and run scripts/ask.py
    --examples --quiet against it 2–3 minutes beforehand. That warms the prompt
    cache, which lasts about 5 minutes.
  - I'd avoid "keep-alive pinger" workarounds on the free tier.

  Code changes

  1. backend/app.py: serve frontend/dist at /; add the access-code check, the
     rate limits and the optional spend cap to /chat; production fails fast
     without ACCESS_CODE.
  2. backend/settings.py: the ANALYSIS_LAST_YEAR override.
  3. Frontend: the access-code screen, the header on each request, and messages
     for 401 and 429.
  4. Dockerfile: one stage builds the frontend with Node, the other runs Python.
     Plus render.yaml with the build and start settings; the secrets are
     entered in the dashboard.
  5. .gitignore: allow data/weather.db.
  6. scripts/smoke_test.py --url … --code …: checks a deployed URL.

  How we test it before you send the link

  1. Unit tests (free): 401 without or with a wrong code; 200 with the right
     one; 429 over each limit; the spend cap; refusing to start in production
     without a code; static files served; the period pinned.
  2. Production mode locally (about $0.02): I can't build the Docker image,
     since Docker isn't installed. So I'll build the frontend and run uvicorn
     with APP_ENV=production on one port, then go through the code screen and
     ask a real question plus a follow-up in the browser, with screenshots.
  3. Deploy on Render's free tier ($0 hosting): the Docker build is tested there
     for the first time. The smoke test then checks:
     - /health responds
     - /chat returns 401 without the code, called directly with no UI involved
     - 1 question and a follow-up in the same session (about $0.03)
     - how long a cold start takes
  4. Switch to Starter, re-run the smoke test, check the key isn't exposed, then
     you send the link and the code.

  Once it's live

  - README: a "Live demo" section with the URL (the code is shared privately,
    never in the repo) and a note on how to warm the cache before a demo.
  - Limitations, replacing "not deployed":
    - access is one shared code, with no user accounts
    - per-IP and global rate limits, plus a spend cap
    - sessions are lost on restart or redeploy
    - the data is a fixed snapshot of 2016–2025
    - a single instance

  Decisions for you:
  1. One service on Render (my recommendation), or the Vercel + Render split?
  2. Free tier for testing, then Starter while reviewers have the link?
  3. Add the daily spend cap?
  4. Rate limits as proposed (20 per hour per IP, 60 per hour total)?

  Sources:

    (https://render.com/docs/free?utm=airanks.net)
  - Render — Platforms with a real free tier in 2026 (https://render.com/article
    s/platforms-with-a-real-free-tier-for-developers-in-2026.md)
  - Render Pricing 2026 (third-party, Starter ~$7) (https://www.activepieces.com
    /blog/render-pricing-2026-free-tier-workers-total-cost.md)
  - Railway — Pricing plans (https://docs.railway.com/pricing/plans.md)
  - Railway — Pricing FAQs (https://docs.railway.com/reference/pricing/faqs)
  - Vercel — Function duration limits
    (https://vercel.com/docs/functions/configuring-functions/duration.md)
  - Vercel — Function limits (https://vercel.com/docs/functions/limits)

       7 +export function storedCode(): string | null {

       9 +    return sessionStorage.getItem(CODE_KEY)                    
      10 +  } catch {

         +thing breaks

      13 +}

      18 +    else sessionStorage.removeItem(CODE_KEY)                   
      19 +  } catch {                                                    
      20 +    /* not critical */                                         
      21 +  }                                                            
      22 +}

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

● Everything is built, tested and pushed to GitHub (28b92ed). I've stopped
  before the actual deploy, as you asked. All 84 tests pass, including 14 new
  security tests. The local production-mode check passed all 12 smoke checks,
  and the access screen works in a real browser in both themes.

  What I built

  - Access code, checked by the backend on every /chat request, so calling the
    API directly gets the same 401 as the UI. APP_ENV=production refuses to
    start without a code of at least 8 characters. A small /access endpoint
    checks a code without calling the model, so the screen can give feedback
    immediately.
  - Limits:
    - 40 questions per hour per IP
    - 60 per hour across everyone
    - 10 wrong codes per hour per IP, then that IP is blocked for an hour
    - $1 per day of Claude spend, counted from real token usage
  - Visitor IP: taken from True-Client-IP, never from X-Forwarded-For, which a
    caller can forge on Render. If that header is missing, everyone shares one
    limit: stricter, never looser.
  - Two hardening fixes:
    - Sessions only resume server-issued IDs, and are capped at 500.
    - The analysis period is pinned (ANALYSIS_LAST_YEAR=2025), so the deployed
      database won't break on 1 January.
  - One service: the backend serves the built UI. The Dockerfile builds the UI
    with Node, then the Python image, and .dockerignore keeps .env out of it.
    render.yaml sets sync: false for the two secrets. The 3.9 MB database
    snapshot is committed.
  - scripts/smoke_test.py for the live URL, and ask.py --code for warming the
    cache before the demo. docs/DEPLOY.md has the steps below.

  What to set up on Render

  1. Account: sign up at render.com. "Sign up with GitHub" is simplest.
  2. Connect the repo: Dashboard → New → Blueprint → connect GitHub. When GitHub
     asks for access, you can grant it to only wheather-risk-agent. Pick the
     repo and branch main, and Render finds render.yaml.
  3. Environment variables: Render asks for exactly two.
     - ANTHROPIC_API_KEY: your key.
     - ACCESS_CODE: the reviewers' code. Make it 12 characters or more and
       generate it in a password manager. Don't paste it into this chat: the
       session exports go into the public repo.

     Everything else is already in render.yaml: APP_ENV=production,
     ANALYSIS_LAST_YEAR=2025, AGENT_MODEL=claude-sonnet-5-5,
     AGENT_EFFORT=medium, the three rate limits and DAILY_SPEND_CAP_USD=1.00.
  4. Apply. It builds on the free plan; the first build takes a few minutes.
  5. Test it yourself, so the code never passes through me:
  .venv\Scripts\python scripts\smoke_test.py --url
  https://<your-service>.onrender.com --code <ACCESS_CODE>
     Paste me the output; it never prints the code. Check the line rate limiter
     keys on: true-client-ip means each visitor has their own limit, while
     socket means everyone shares one (safe, but 40 per hour in total).
  6. Before sending the link: Settings → Instance type → Starter, then run the
     smoke test again.

  Good to know

  - Render redeploys on every push to main. A redeploy resets the sessions, the
    rate limits and the day's spend counter.
  - A backstop worth having: if your Anthropic Console account lets you set a
    monthly spend limit, set one too.
  - The repo name is wheather-risk-agent, with an extra "h". Reviewers will see
    it in the GitHub link; renaming it on GitHub before connecting Render is
    easy, if you'd like to.
  - The README still says "no authentication / not deployed". As agreed, I'll
    update it and the limitations once the service is live.

  Once it's up, send me the URL and the smoke-test output, and I'll do the
  README and limitations update.

  Sources:
  - Render — Blueprint YAML Reference (https://render.com/docs/blueprint-spec)
  - Render — Blueprints (infrastructure as code)
    (https://render.com/docs/infrastructure-as-code)
  - Render feedback — X-Forwarded-For is appended, not reset
    (https://feedback.render.com/features/p/send-the-correct-xforwardedfor)
  - Rails on Render — True-Client-IP via Cloudflare (third-party)
    (https://hn.jakechampion.name/reader/https://rohitpaulk.com/articles/render-
    rails-remote-ip)

  Exposure check: no API key anywhere
  - Repository: none of the 10 commits on GitHub contains a key, an
    ANTHROPIC_API_KEY= with a value, or an ACCESS_CODE= with a value. .env was
    never committed and is still ignored.
  - Live service: the shipped JavaScript and CSS contain no key, nor even the
    word "ANTHROPIC". /chat returns 401 with no code or a wrong one, and /.env
    returns 404. Rate limits are applied per visitor (true-client-ip), not
    shared by everyone.
  - One finding, now fixed: FastAPI's interactive API docs (/docs and
    /openapi.json) were public. They held no secrets, but they're now switched
    off in production, with a test. Both return 404 on the live service.

  1. README "Live demo" section
  - the URL
  - your cold-start note, word for word, at the top of the section
  - the access code is shared privately and never in the repo
  - the usage limits in plain words
  - a link to docs/DEPLOY.md

  2. Limitations, in the README and ARCHITECTURE § 10
  "Not deployed" is replaced by what's actually running:
  - one shared access code, with no user accounts
  - per-IP (40 per hour) and global (60 per hour) rate limits
  - a $1/day spend cap
  - free-tier cold starts
  - sessions and the limit counters are lost on restart or redeploy, so a
    redeploy also resets the day's spend
  - the fixed 2016–2025 data snapshot
  - a single instance, which can't scale out without a shared store

  ARCHITECTURE § 1 also has a new "Deployment and access control" section, and
  the repo structure now lists the security module, the database snapshot, the
  Dockerfile, render.yaml and DEPLOY.md.

  3. Decisions log
  - D17: one Render service rather than Vercel plus a separate backend.
  - D18: the free tier, not Starter. It costs nothing, and its only drawback is
    a documented one-minute first load after 15 idle minutes; answers are
    unaffected after that.
  - D19: the access code, limits and spend cap, including why the per-IP limit
    is 40 (reviewers sharing one office IP).

  DEPLOY.md and the render.yaml comment now describe the free tier instead of
  telling you to switch to Starter.
